<ul><li>Some Jews believed God later destroyed Herod Antipas' army as a punishment, because he had unjustly executed John the Baptist.</li></ul>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<h2><span id="In_Josephus.27_Antiquities_of_the_Jews"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="In_Josephus'_Antiquities_of_the_Jews">In Josephus' <i>Antiquities of the Jews</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: In Josephus&#039; Antiquities of the Jews">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>An account of John the Baptist is found in all extant manuscripts of the <i><a href="/wiki/Antiquities_of_the_Jews" title="Antiquities of the Jews">Antiquities of the Jews</a></i> (book 18, chapter 5, 2) by <a href="/wiki/Flavius_Josephus" class="mw-redirect" title="Flavius Josephus">Flavius Josephus</a> (37–100):<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Now some of the Jews thought that the destruction of Herod's [Antipas's] army came from God, and that very justly, as a punishment of what he did against John, that was called the Baptist: for Herod slew him, who was a good man, and commanded the Jews to exercise virtue, both as to righteousness towards one another, and piety towards God, and so to come to baptism; for that the washing [with water] would be acceptable to him, if they made use of it, not in order to the putting away [or the remission] of some sins [only], but for the purification of the body; supposing still that the soul was thoroughly purified beforehand by righteousness. Now when [many] others came in crowds about him, for they were very greatly moved [or pleased] by hearing his words, Herod, who feared lest the great influence John had over the people might put it into his power and inclination to raise a rebellion, (for they seemed ready to do any thing he should advise,) thought it best, by putting him to death, to prevent any mischief he might cause, and not bring himself into difficulties, by sparing a man who might make him repent of it when it would be too late. Accordingly he was sent a prisoner, out of Herod's suspicious temper, to <a href="/wiki/Machaerus" title="Machaerus">Macherus</a>, the castle I before mentioned, and was there put to death. Now the Jews had an opinion that the destruction of this army was sent as a punishment upon Herod, and a mark of God's displeasure to him.<sup id="cite_ref-Josephus18a_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Josephus18a-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>According to this passage, the execution of John was blamed for the defeat Herod suffered. Some have claimed that this passage indicates that John died near the time of the destruction of Herod's army in AD 36. However, in a different passage, Josephus states that the end of Herod's marriage with Aretas' daughter (after which John was killed) was only the beginning of hostilities between Herod and Aretas, which later escalated into the battle.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
Biblical scholar <a href="/wiki/John_Dominic_Crossan" title="John Dominic Crossan">John Dominic Crossan</a> differentiates between Josephus's account of John and Jesus, saying, "John had a monopoly, but Jesus had a franchise." To get baptized, Crossan writes, a person went only to John; to stop the movement one only needed to stop John (therefore his movement ended with his death). Jesus invited all to come and see how he and his companions had already accepted the government of God, entered it and were living it. Such a communal praxis was not just for himself, but could survive without him, unlike John's movement.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup></p><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Nabi_Yahya_Mosque,_Sebastia,_c._1920.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Nabi_Yahya_Mosque%2C_Sebastia%2C_c._1920.jpg/220px-Nabi_Yahya_Mosque%2C_Sebastia%2C_c._1920.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="247" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Nabi_Yahya_Mosque%2C_Sebastia%2C_c._1920.jpg/330px-Nabi_Yahya_Mosque%2C_Sebastia%2C_c._1920.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/e6/Nabi_Yahya_Mosque%2C_Sebastia%2C_c._1920.jpg 2x" data-file-width="440" data-file-height="494" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Nabi_Yahya_Mosque,_Sebastia,_c._1920.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Nabi_Yahya_Mosque" title="Nabi Yahya Mosque">Nabi Yahya Mosque</a>, the traditional burial site of John the Baptist, in <a href="/wiki/Sebastia_(Palestine)" class="mw-redirect" title="Sebastia (Palestine)">Sebastia</a>, near <a href="/wiki/Nablus" title="Nablus">Nablus</a></div></div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Relics">Relics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Relics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Beheading_of_Saint_John_the_Baptist#Relics" class="mw-redirect" title="Beheading of Saint John the Baptist">Beheading of Saint John the Baptist §&#160;Relics</a></div>
<p>Matthew 14:12 records that "his disciples came and took away [John's] body and buried it."<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> Theologian <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Benson" title="Joseph Benson">Joseph Benson</a> refers to a belief that they managed to do so because "it seems that the body had been thrown over the prison walls, without burial, probably by order of Herodias."<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:MonasteryOfSt.ohnInTheWildernessFeb122022_04.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/MonasteryOfSt.ohnInTheWildernessFeb122022_04.jpg/220px-MonasteryOfSt.ohnInTheWildernessFeb122022_04.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/MonasteryOfSt.ohnInTheWildernessFeb122022_04.jpg/330px-MonasteryOfSt.ohnInTheWildernessFeb122022_04.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/MonasteryOfSt.ohnInTheWildernessFeb122022_04.jpg/440px-MonasteryOfSt.ohnInTheWildernessFeb122022_04.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:MonasteryOfSt.ohnInTheWildernessFeb122022_04.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Monastery_of_Saint_John_in_the_Wilderness" title="Monastery of Saint John in the Wilderness">Monastery of Saint John in the Wilderness</a></div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_fate_of_his_head">The fate of his head</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: The fate of his head">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>What became of the head of John the Baptist is difficult to determine. Ancient historians <a href="/wiki/Josephus" title="Josephus">Josephus</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nikephoros_Kallistos_Xanthopoulos" title="Nikephoros Kallistos Xanthopoulos">Nicephorus</a><sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Symeon_the_Metaphrast" title="Symeon the Metaphrast">Symeon Metaphrastes</a> assumed that <a href="/wiki/Herodias" title="Herodias">Herodias</a> had it buried in the fortress of <a href="/wiki/Machaerus" title="Machaerus">Machaerus</a>.
</p><p>An <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Orthodox Christian</a> tradition holds that, after buried, the head was discovered by John's followers and was taken to the <a href="/wiki/Mount_of_Olives" title="Mount of Olives">Mount of Olives</a>, where it was twice buried and discovered, the latter events giving rise to the Orthodox feast of the <a href="/wiki/Beheading_of_John_the_Baptist#Related_feasts" title="Beheading of John the Baptist">First and Second Finding of the Head of St. John the Baptist</a>. Other writers say that it was interred in Herod's palace at Jerusalem; there it was found during the reign of <a href="/wiki/Constantine_the_Great" title="Constantine the Great">Constantine</a>, and thence secretly taken to <a href="/wiki/Homs" title="Homs">Emesa (modern Homs, in Syria)</a>, where it was concealed, the place remaining unknown for years, until it was manifested by <a href="/wiki/Revelation" title="Revelation">revelation</a> in 452,<sup id="cite_ref-Dumper_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dumper-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> an event celebrated in the Orthodox Church as the <a href="/wiki/Beheading_of_John_the_Baptist#Related_feasts" title="Beheading of John the Baptist">Third Finding</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:The_shrine_of_John_the_Baptist,_Damascus,_April_2008.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/The_shrine_of_John_the_Baptist%2C_Damascus%2C_April_2008.jpg/220px-The_shrine_of_John_the_Baptist%2C_Damascus%2C_April_2008.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/The_shrine_of_John_the_Baptist%2C_Damascus%2C_April_2008.jpg/330px-The_shrine_of_John_the_Baptist%2C_Damascus%2C_April_2008.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/The_shrine_of_John_the_Baptist%2C_Damascus%2C_April_2008.jpg/440px-The_shrine_of_John_the_Baptist%2C_Damascus%2C_April_2008.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1251" data-file-height="841" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:The_shrine_of_John_the_Baptist,_Damascus,_April_2008.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Shrine of John the Baptist in the <a href="/wiki/Umayyad_Mosque" title="Umayyad Mosque">Umayyad Mosque</a>, which purportedly houses John the Baptist's head</div></div></div>
<p>Two <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a> churches and one mosque claim to have the head of John the Baptist: the <a href="/wiki/Umayyad_Mosque" title="Umayyad Mosque">Umayyad Mosque</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Damascus" title="Damascus">Damascus</a> (<a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a>); the church of <a href="/wiki/San_Silvestro_in_Capite" title="San Silvestro in Capite">San Silvestro in Capite</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>; and <a href="/wiki/Amiens_Cathedral" title="Amiens Cathedral">Amiens Cathedral</a>, in <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> (the French king would have had it brought from the Holy Land after the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Crusade" title="Fourth Crusade">Fourth Crusade</a>). A fourth claim is made by the <a href="/wiki/Munich_Residenz" title="Munich Residenz">Residenz Museum</a> in Munich, Germany, which keeps a reliquary containing what the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Wittelsbach" title="House of Wittelsbach">Wittelsbach</a> rulers of Bavaria believed to be the head of Saint John.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Right_hand_relics">Right hand relics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Right hand relics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Armenian_Chinsurah_2.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Armenian_Chinsurah_2.jpg/220px-Armenian_Chinsurah_2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Armenian_Chinsurah_2.jpg/330px-Armenian_Chinsurah_2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Armenian_Chinsurah_2.jpg/440px-Armenian_Chinsurah_2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="402" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Armenian_Chinsurah_2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <a href="/wiki/Kolkata" title="Kolkata">Kolkata</a> <a href="/wiki/Armenians_in_India" title="Armenians in India">Armenian</a> kisses the hand of St John the Baptist at <a href="/wiki/Chinsurah" class="mw-redirect" title="Chinsurah">Chinsurah</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>An <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Orthodox Christian</a> <a href="/wiki/Cetinje_Monastery" title="Cetinje Monastery">monastery</a> in <a href="/wiki/Cetinje_Monastery" title="Cetinje Monastery">Cetinje</a>, <a href="/wiki/Montenegro" title="Montenegro">Montenegro</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a> Cathedral of <a href="/wiki/Siena" title="Siena">Siena</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>, both claim to have John the Baptist's right arm and hand, with which he baptised Jesus.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-bones_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bones-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> According to the Catholic account, in 1464 <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_II" title="Pope Pius II">Pope Pius II</a> donated what was identified as the right arm and hand of John the Baptist to the Siena Cathedral.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-101">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> The donation charter identifies the relic as "the arm of blessed John the Baptist. And this is the very arm that baptized the Lord." The relic is displayed on the high altar of the <a href="/wiki/Siena_Cathedral" title="Siena Cathedral">Siena Cathedral</a> annually in June.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Topkap%C4%B1_Palace" title="Topkapı Palace">Topkapi Palace</a>, in Istanbul, claims to have John's right hand index finger.<sup id="cite_ref-bones_100-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bones-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Various_relics_and_traditions">Various relics and traditions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Various relics and traditions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span id="Left_hand_.E2.80.93_St._John_the_Baptist_Church_of_Chinsurah_.28India.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Left_hand_–_St._John_the_Baptist_Church_of_Chinsurah_(India)">Left hand – St. John the Baptist Church of Chinsurah (India)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Left hand – St. John the Baptist Church of Chinsurah (India)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The saint's left hand is allegedly preserved in the Armenian Apostolic Church of St. John at Chinsurah, <a href="/wiki/West_Bengal" title="West Bengal">West Bengal</a>, in India, where each year on "Chinsurah Day" in January it blesses the <a href="/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church" title="Armenian Apostolic Church">Armenian Christians</a> of <a href="/wiki/Kolkata" title="Kolkata">Calcutta</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Decapitation_cloth">Decapitation cloth</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Decapitation cloth">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The decapitation cloth of Saint John, the cloth which covered his head after his execution, is said to be kept at the <a href="/wiki/Aachen_Cathedral" title="Aachen Cathedral">Aachen Cathedral</a>, in Germany.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Historic_Armenia">Historic Armenia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Historic Armenia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Surp_Garabed_Vank_(Hampikian,_1923).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Surp_Garabed_Vank_%28Hampikian%2C_1923%29.jpg/220px-Surp_Garabed_Vank_%28Hampikian%2C_1923%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="153" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Surp_Garabed_Vank_%28Hampikian%2C_1923%29.jpg/330px-Surp_Garabed_Vank_%28Hampikian%2C_1923%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Surp_Garabed_Vank_%28Hampikian%2C_1923%29.jpg/440px-Surp_Garabed_Vank_%28Hampikian%2C_1923%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="945" data-file-height="658" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Surp_Garabed_Vank_(Hampikian,_1923).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Saint_Karapet_Monastery" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Karapet Monastery">Saint Karapet Monastery</a>, where Armenian tradition holds that his remains were laid to rest by Gregory the Illuminator<sup id="cite_ref-Kharatyan_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kharatyan-104">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Avetisyan_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Avetisyan-105">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>According to Armenian tradition, the remains of John the Baptist would in some point have been transferred by <a href="/wiki/Gregory_the_Illuminator" title="Gregory the Illuminator">Gregory the Illuminator</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Surb_Karapet_Monastery" title="Surb Karapet Monastery">Saint Karapet Armenian Monastery</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Kharatyan_104-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kharatyan-104">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Avetisyan_105-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Avetisyan-105">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Bulgaria">Bulgaria</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Bulgaria">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 2010, bones were discovered in the ruins of a Bulgarian church in the St. John the Forerunner Monastery (4th–17th centuries) on the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a> island of <a href="/wiki/St._Ivan_Island" title="St. Ivan Island">Sveti Ivan</a> (Saint John) and two years later, after DNA and radio carbon testing proved the bones belonged to a Middle Eastern man who lived in the 1st century AD, scientists said that the remains could conceivably have belonged to John the Baptist.<sup id="cite_ref-NG_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NG-106">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> The remains, found in a reliquarium, are presently kept in the Sts. Cyril and Methodius Cathedral in <a href="/wiki/Sozopol" title="Sozopol">Sozopol</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-NG_106-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NG-106">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Egypt">Egypt</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Egypt">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:St_John_the_Baptists_tomb.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/St_John_the_Baptists_tomb.JPG/220px-St_John_the_Baptists_tomb.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/St_John_the_Baptists_tomb.JPG/330px-St_John_the_Baptists_tomb.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/St_John_the_Baptists_tomb.JPG/440px-St_John_the_Baptists_tomb.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="1944" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:St_John_the_Baptists_tomb.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tomb of Saint John the Baptist at a <a href="/wiki/Coptic_Orthodox_Church_of_Alexandria" class="mw-redirect" title="Coptic Orthodox Church of Alexandria">Coptic</a> <a href="/wiki/Monastery" title="Monastery">monastery</a> in <a href="/wiki/Lower_Egypt" title="Lower Egypt">Lower Egypt</a>. The bones of Saint John the Baptist were said to have been found here.</div></div></div>
<p>A crypt and relics said to be John's and mentioned in 11th- and 16th-century manuscripts, were discovered in 1969 during restoration of the Church of St. Macarius at the <a href="/wiki/Monastery_of_Saint_Macarius_the_Great" title="Monastery of Saint Macarius the Great">Monastery of Saint Macarius the Great</a> in <a href="/wiki/Scetes" class="mw-redirect" title="Scetes">Scetes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Egypt" title="Egypt">Egypt</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Stmacariusmonastery.org_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stmacariusmonastery.org-109">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Coptic Christian Orthodox Church also claim to hold the relics of Saint John the Baptist. These are to be found in a monastery in Lower Egypt between Cairo and Alexandria. It is possible, with permission from the monks, to see the original tomb where the remains were found.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="Probably the same as above – Scetes is indeed between Cairo and Alex. (October 2020)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Nagorno-Karabakh">Nagorno-Karabakh</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Nagorno-Karabakh">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Additional relics are claimed to reside in <a href="/wiki/Gandzasar_Monastery" class="mw-redirect" title="Gandzasar Monastery">Gandzasar Monastery</a>'s Cathedral of St. John the Baptist, in <a href="/wiki/Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Nagorno-Karabakh">Nagorno-Karabakh</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Purported_left_finger_bone">Purported left finger bone</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Purported left finger bone">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The bone of one of John the Baptist's left fingers is said to be at the <a href="/wiki/Nelson-Atkins_Museum_of_Art" title="Nelson-Atkins Museum of Art">Nelson-Atkins Museum of Art</a> in <a href="/wiki/Kansas_City,_Missouri" title="Kansas City, Missouri">Kansas City, Missouri</a>. It is held in a Gothic-style <a href="/wiki/Monstrance" title="Monstrance">monstrance</a> made of <a href="/wiki/Silver-gilt" title="Silver-gilt">gilded silver</a> that dates back to 14th century <a href="/wiki/Lower_Saxony" title="Lower Saxony">Lower Saxony</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Halifax.2C_England"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Halifax,_England">Halifax, England</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Halifax, England">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Another obscure claim relates to the town of <a href="/wiki/Halifax,_West_Yorkshire" title="Halifax, West Yorkshire">Halifax</a> in West Yorkshire, United Kingdom, where, as patron saint of the town, the Baptist's head appears on the official coat-of-arms.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> One legend (among others) bases the etymology of the town's place-name on "halig" (holy) and "fax" (hair), claiming that a relic of the head, or face, of John the Baptist once existed in the town.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Religious_views">Religious views</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Religious views">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Christianity">Christianity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Christianity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Gospel" title="Gospel">Gospels</a> describe John the Baptist as having had a specific role ordained by <a href="/wiki/God_in_Christianity" title="God in Christianity">God</a> as forerunner or precursor of <a href="/wiki/Jesus_in_Christianity" title="Jesus in Christianity">Jesus</a>, who was the foretold <a href="/wiki/Messiah" title="Messiah">Messiah</a>. The New Testament Gospels speak of this role. In Luke 1:17 the role of John is referred to as being "to turn the hearts of the fathers to the children, and the disobedient to the wisdom of the just; to make ready a people prepared for the Lord."<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> In Luke 1:76 as "thou shalt go before the face of the Lord to prepare his ways"<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> and in Luke 1:77 as being "To give knowledge of salvation unto his people by the remission of their sins."<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are several passages within the <a href="/wiki/Old_Testament" title="Old Testament">Old Testament</a> which are interpreted by Christians as being <a href="/wiki/Prophecy" title="Prophecy">prophetic</a> of John the Baptist in this role. These include a passage in the <a href="/wiki/Book_of_Malachi" title="Book of Malachi">Book of Malachi</a> that refers to a prophet who would "prepare the way of the Lord":
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Behold, I will send my messenger, and he shall prepare the way before me: and the Lord, whom ye seek, shall suddenly come to his temple, even the messenger of the covenant, whom ye delight in: behold, he shall come, saith the L<span class="smallcaps" style="font-size:83%">ORD</span> of hosts.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8201;<cite>Malachi 3:1<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup></cite></div></blockquote>
<p>Also at the end of the next chapter in Malachi 4:5–6 it says,
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Behold, I will send you Elijah the prophet before the coming of the great and dreadful day of the L<span class="smallcaps" style="font-size:83%">ORD</span>: And he shall turn the heart of the fathers to the children, and the heart of the children to their fathers, lest I come and smite the earth with a curse.</p></blockquote>
<p>The Jews of Jesus' day expected Elijah to come before the Messiah; indeed, some present day Jews continue to await Elijah's coming as well, as in the Cup of Elijah the Prophet in the <a href="/wiki/Passover_Seder" title="Passover Seder">Passover Seder</a>. This is why the disciples ask Jesus in Matthew 17:10, "Why then say the scribes that Elias must first come?"<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> The disciples are then told by Jesus that Elijah came in the person of John the Baptist,
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Jesus replied, "To be sure, Elijah comes and will restore all things. But I tell you, Elijah has already come, and they did not recognize him, but have done to him everything they wished. In the same way the Son of Man is going to suffer at their hands." Then the disciples understood that he was talking to them about John the Baptist.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8201;<cite>Matthew 17:11–13 (see also 11:14: "...if you are willing to accept it, he is Elijah who was to come.")</cite></div></blockquote>
<p>These passages are applied to John in the <a href="/wiki/Synoptic_Gospels" title="Synoptic Gospels">Synoptic Gospels</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> But where Matthew specifically identifies John the Baptist as Elijah's spiritual successor,<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> the gospels of Mark and Luke are silent on the matter. The <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_John" title="Gospel of John">Gospel of John</a> states that John the Baptist denied that he was Elijah.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Now this was John's testimony when the Jews of Jerusalem sent priests and Levites to ask him who he was. He did not deny, but confessed freely, "I am not the Christ." They asked him, "Then who are you? Are you Elijah?" He said, "I am not." "Are you the Prophet?" He answered, "No."</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8201;<cite>John 1:19–21</cite></div></blockquote>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Influence_on_Paul">Influence on Paul</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Influence on Paul">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Many scholars believe there was contact between the early church in the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Age" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Age">Apostolic Age</a> and what is called the "<a href="/wiki/Qumran" title="Qumran">Qumran</a>-<a href="/wiki/Essene" class="mw-redirect" title="Essene">Essene</a> community".<sup id="cite_ref-EDSS_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EDSS-123">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Dead_Sea_Scrolls" title="Dead Sea Scrolls">Dead Sea Scrolls</a> were found at Qumran, which the majority of historians and archaeologists identify as an Essene settlement.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> John the Baptist is thought to have been either an Essene or "associated" with the community at Khirbet Qumran. According to the <a href="/wiki/Book_of_Acts" class="mw-redirect" title="Book of Acts">Book of Acts</a>, Paul met some "disciples of John" in <a href="/wiki/Ephesus" title="Ephesus">Ephesus</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Catholic_Church">Catholic Church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Catholic Church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cappella_tornabuoni,_12,_Nascita_di_san_giovanni_battista.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Cappella_tornabuoni%2C_12%2C_Nascita_di_san_giovanni_battista.jpg/220px-Cappella_tornabuoni%2C_12%2C_Nascita_di_san_giovanni_battista.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Cappella_tornabuoni%2C_12%2C_Nascita_di_san_giovanni_battista.jpg/330px-Cappella_tornabuoni%2C_12%2C_Nascita_di_san_giovanni_battista.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Cappella_tornabuoni%2C_12%2C_Nascita_di_san_giovanni_battista.jpg/440px-Cappella_tornabuoni%2C_12%2C_Nascita_di_san_giovanni_battista.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2885" data-file-height="1967" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cappella_tornabuoni,_12,_Nascita_di_san_giovanni_battista.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Birth of John the Baptist</i>, <a href="/wiki/Cappella_Tornabuoni" class="mw-redirect" title="Cappella Tornabuoni">Cappella Tornabuoni</a></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> commemorates Saint John the Baptist on two feast days:
</p>
<ul><li>24 June – <a href="/wiki/Nativity_of_Saint_John_the_Baptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Nativity of Saint John the Baptist">Nativity of Saint John the Baptist</a></li>
<li>29 August – <a href="/wiki/Beheading_of_Saint_John_the_Baptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Beheading of Saint John the Baptist">Beheading of Saint John the Baptist</a></li></ul>
<p>According to <a href="/wiki/Frederick_George_Holweck" title="Frederick George Holweck">Frederick Holweck</a>, at the <a href="/wiki/Visitation_(Christianity)" title="Visitation (Christianity)">Visitation</a> of the Blessed Virgin Mary to his mother Elizabeth, as recounted in Luke 1:39–57, John, sensing the presence of his Jesus, upon the arrival of Mary, leaped in the womb of his mother; he was then cleansed from original sin and filled with the grace of God.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> In her <i>Treatise of Prayer</i>, Saint <a href="/wiki/Catherine_of_Siena" title="Catherine of Siena">Catherine of Siena</a> includes a brief altercation with the <a href="/wiki/Devil_in_Christianity" title="Devil in Christianity">Devil</a> regarding her fight due to the Devil attempting to lure her with <a href="/wiki/Vanity" title="Vanity">vanity</a> and <a href="/wiki/Flattery" title="Flattery">flattery</a>. Speaking in the first person, Catherine responds to the Devil with the following words:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>[...] humiliation of yourself, and you answered the Devil with these words: "Wretch that I am! John the Baptist never sinned and was sanctified in his mother's womb. And I have committed so many sins [...]"</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8201;<cite><a href="/wiki/Catherine_of_Siena" title="Catherine of Siena">Catherine of Siena</a>, <i>A Treatise of Prayer</i>, 1370.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup></cite></div></blockquote>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Eastern_Christianity">Eastern Christianity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Eastern Christianity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Meister_von_Gracanica_(I)_001.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Meister_von_Gracanica_%28I%29_001.jpg/220px-Meister_von_Gracanica_%28I%29_001.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="263" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Meister_von_Gracanica_%28I%29_001.jpg/330px-Meister_von_Gracanica_%28I%29_001.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Meister_von_Gracanica_%28I%29_001.jpg/440px-Meister_von_Gracanica_%28I%29_001.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2024" data-file-height="2415" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Meister_von_Gracanica_(I)_001.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Serbo-Byzantine_architecture" title="Serbo-Byzantine architecture">Serbo-Byzantine</a> fresco from <a href="/wiki/Gra%C4%8Danica_Monastery" title="Gračanica Monastery">Gračanica Monastery</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kosovo" title="Kosovo">Kosovo</a>, <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;1235</span></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Catholic_Churches" title="Eastern Catholic Churches">Eastern Catholic Churches</a> and <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox</a> faithful believe that John was the last of the <a href="/wiki/Old_Testament" title="Old Testament">Old Testament</a> <a href="/wiki/Prophet" title="Prophet">prophets</a>, thus serving as a bridge between that period of <a href="/wiki/Revelation" title="Revelation">revelation</a> and the <a href="/wiki/New_Covenant" title="New Covenant">New Covenant</a>. They also teach that, following his death, John descended into <a href="/wiki/Christian_views_on_Hades" title="Christian views on Hades">Hades</a> and there once more preached that Jesus the Messiah was coming, so he was the Forerunner of Christ in death as he had been in life. Eastern Catholic and Orthodox churches will often have an <a href="/wiki/Icon" title="Icon">icon</a> of Saint John the Baptist in a place of honor on the <a href="/wiki/Iconostasis" title="Iconostasis">iconostasis</a>, and he is frequently mentioned during the <a href="/wiki/Divine_Services" class="mw-redirect" title="Divine Services">Divine Services</a>. Every Tuesday throughout the year is dedicated to his memory.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a> remembers Saint John the Forerunner on six separate feast days, listed here in order in which they occur during the <a href="/wiki/Liturgical_year" title="Liturgical year">church year</a> (which begins on 1 September):
</p>
<ul><li>23 September – <a href="/w/index.php?title=%D0%97%D0%B0%D1%87%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B5_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B8&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Зачатие Иоанна Предтечи (page does not exist)">Conception of the Honorable Glorious Prophet, Forerunner and Baptist John</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%97%D0%B0%D1%87%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B5_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B8" class="extiw" title="ru:Зачатие Иоанна Предтечи">ru</a>&#93;</span><sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>12 October – <a href="/wiki/Translation" title="Translation">Translation</a> from <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_Military_Order_of_Malta" title="Sovereign Military Order of Malta">Malta</a> to <a href="/wiki/Gatchina" title="Gatchina">Gatchina</a>: of <a href="/wiki/True_Cross" title="True Cross">a Particle of the Life Giving Cross</a>, <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Philermos" title="Our Lady of Philermos">the Filersk Icon of the Mother of God</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Relic" title="Relic">relic</a> of the <a href="/w/index.php?title=%D0%94%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BD%D0%B8%D1%86%D0%B0_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9A%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BB%D1%8F&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Десница Иоанна Крестителя (page does not exist)">Right Hand of John the Baptist</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%94%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BD%D0%B8%D1%86%D0%B0_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9A%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BB%D1%8F" class="extiw" title="ru:Десница Иоанна Крестителя">ru</a>&#93;</span></li>
<li>7 January – <a href="/w/index.php?title=%D0%A1%D0%BE%D0%B1%D0%BE%D1%80_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B8&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Собор Иоанна Предтечи (page does not exist)">Synaxis of the Holy Glorious Prophet, Forerunner and Baptist John</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%A1%D0%BE%D0%B1%D0%BE%D1%80_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B8" class="extiw" title="ru:Собор Иоанна Предтечи">ru</a>&#93;</span>. This is his main feast day, immediately after <a href="/wiki/Epiphany_(feast)" class="mw-redirect" title="Epiphany (feast)">Theophany</a> on 6 January (7 January also commemorates the transfer of the relic of the right hand of John the Baptist from <a href="/wiki/Antioch" title="Antioch">Antioch</a> to <a href="/wiki/Constantinople" title="Constantinople">Constantinople</a> in 956)</li>
<li>24 February – <a href="/w/index.php?title=%D0%9E%D0%B1%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B5_%D0%B3%D0%BB%D0%B0%D0%B2%D1%8B_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B8&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Обретение главы Иоанна Предтечи (page does not exist)">First and second finding of the Honorable Head of the Holy Glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist of the Lord, John</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9E%D0%B1%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B5_%D0%B3%D0%BB%D0%B0%D0%B2%D1%8B_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B8" class="extiw" title="ru:Обретение главы Иоанна Предтечи">ru</a>&#93;</span></li>
<li>25 May – <a href="/w/index.php?title=%D0%9E%D0%B1%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B5_%D0%B3%D0%BB%D0%B0%D0%B2%D1%8B_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B8&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Обретение главы Иоанна Предтечи (page does not exist)">Third Finding of the Honorable Head of the Holy Glorious Prophet, Forerunner and Baptist John</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9E%D0%B1%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B5_%D0%B3%D0%BB%D0%B0%D0%B2%D1%8B_%D0%98%D0%BE%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B8" class="extiw" title="ru:Обретение главы Иоанна Предтечи">ru</a>&#93;</span></li>
<li>24 June – <a href="/wiki/Nativity_of_Saint_John_the_Baptist#In_Eastern_Christianity" class="mw-redirect" title="Nativity of Saint John the Baptist">Nativity of Saint John the Forerunner</a></li>
<li>29 August – The <a href="/wiki/Beheading_of_John_the_Baptist" title="Beheading of John the Baptist">Beheading of Saint John the Forerunner</a>, a day of strict fast and abstinence from meat and dairy products and foods containing meat or dairy products</li></ul>
<p>In addition to the above, 5 September is the commemoration of <a href="/wiki/Zechariah_(New_Testament_figure)" title="Zechariah (New Testament figure)">Zacharias</a> and <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_(Biblical_person)" class="mw-redirect" title="Elizabeth (Biblical person)">Elizabeth</a>, Saint John's parents.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox Church</a> observes 12 October as the Transfer of the Right Hand of the Forerunner from <a href="/wiki/Malta" title="Malta">Malta</a> to <a href="/wiki/Gatchina" title="Gatchina">Gatchina</a> (1799).
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints">Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p><a href="/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints" title="The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints">The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints</a> teaches that modern revelation confirms the biblical account of John and also makes known additional events in his ministry. According to this belief, John was "ordained by the angel of God" when he was eight days old "to overthrow the kingdom of the Jews" and to prepare a people for the Lord. Latter-day Saints also believe that "he was baptized while yet in his childhood."<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Joseph_Smith" title="Joseph Smith">Joseph Smith</a> said: "Let us come into New Testament times – so many are ever praising the Lord and His apostles. We will commence with John the Baptist. When Herod's edict went forth to destroy the young children, John was about six months older than Jesus, and came under this hellish edict, and Zecharias caused his mother to take him into the mountains, where he was raised on locusts and wild honey. When his father refused to disclose his hiding place, and being the officiating high priest at the Temple that year, was slain by Herod's order, between the porch and the altar, as Jesus said."<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-Day Saints teaches that John the Baptist appeared on the banks of the <a href="/wiki/Susquehanna_River" title="Susquehanna River">Susquehanna River</a> near <a href="/wiki/Harmony_Township,_Susquehanna_County,_Pennsylvania" title="Harmony Township, Susquehanna County, Pennsylvania">Harmony Township, Susquehanna County, Pennsylvania</a>, as a resurrected being to <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Smith" title="Joseph Smith">Joseph Smith</a> and <a href="/wiki/Oliver_Cowdery" title="Oliver Cowdery">Oliver Cowdery</a> on 15 May 1829, and ordained them to the <a href="/wiki/Aaronic_priesthood_(LDS_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Aaronic priesthood (LDS Church)">Aaronic priesthood</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> According to the Church's dispensational view of religious history, John's ministry has operated in three dispensations: he was the last of the prophets under the law of Moses; he was the first of the New Testament prophets; and he was sent to restore the Aaronic priesthood in our day (the <a href="/wiki/Dispensation_of_the_fulness_of_times" title="Dispensation of the fulness of times">dispensation of the fulness of times</a>). Latter-day Saints believe John's ministry was foretold by two prophets whose teachings are included in the <a href="/wiki/Book_of_Mormon" title="Book of Mormon">Book of Mormon</a>: <a href="/wiki/Lehi_(Book_of_Mormon)" class="mw-redirect" title="Lehi (Book of Mormon)">Lehi</a><sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> and his son <a href="/wiki/Nephi,_son_of_Lehi" title="Nephi, son of Lehi">Nephi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Unification_Church">Unification Church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Unification Church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Unification_Church" title="Unification Church">Unification Church</a> teaches that <a href="/wiki/God" title="God">God</a> intended John to help Jesus during his public ministry in Judea. In particular, John should have done everything in his power to persuade the Jewish people that Jesus was the Messiah. He was to become Jesus' main disciple and John's disciples were to become Jesus' disciples. Unfortunately, John did not follow Jesus and continued his own way of baptizing people. Moreover, John also denied that he was Elijah when queried by several Jewish leaders,<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> contradicting Jesus who stated John is Elijah who was to come.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Many Jews therefore could not accept Jesus as the Messiah because John denied being Elijah, as the prophet's appearance was a prerequisite for the Messiah's arrival as stated in Malachi 4:5.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> According to the Unification Church, "John the Baptist was in the position of representing Elijah's physical body, making himself identical with Elijah from the standpoint of their mission."
</p><p>According to Matthew 11:11, Jesus stated "there has not risen one greater than John the Baptist."<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> However, in referring to John's blocking the way of the Jews' understanding of him as the Messiah, Jesus said "yet he who is least in the kingdom of heaven is greater than he." John's failure to follow Jesus became the chief obstacle to the fulfillment of Jesus' mission.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Syrian-Egyptian_Gnosticism">Syrian-Egyptian Gnosticism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Syrian-Egyptian Gnosticism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Among the early Judeo-Christian Gnostics the <a href="/wiki/Ebionites" title="Ebionites">Ebionites</a> held that John, along with Jesus and <a href="/wiki/James_the_Just" class="mw-redirect" title="James the Just">James the Just</a> – all of whom they revered – were vegetarians.<sup id="cite_ref-Verheyden_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Verheyden-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ehrman_2003_on_Gospel_of_the_Ebionites_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ehrman_2003_on_Gospel_of_the_Ebionites-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Mead_2007_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mead_2007-150">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Epiphanius_of_Salamis" title="Epiphanius of Salamis">Epiphanius of Salamis</a> records that this group had amended their <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_Matthew" title="Gospel of Matthew">Gospel of Matthew</a> – known today as the <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_the_Ebionites" title="Gospel of the Ebionites">Gospel of the Ebionites</a> – to change where John eats "locusts" to read "honey cakes" or "<a href="/wiki/Manna" title="Manna">manna</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ehrman_2003_Gospel_of_the_Ebionites_translation_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ehrman_2003_Gospel_of_the_Ebionites_translation-152">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mandaeism">Mandaeism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Mandaeism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
