class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs additional citations for <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">verification</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.<br /><small><span class="plainlinks"><i>Find sources:</i>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia&amp;q=%22John+the+Baptist%22">"John the Baptist"</a>&#160;–&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbm=nws&amp;q=%22John+the+Baptist%22+-wikipedia&amp;tbs=ar:1">news</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?&amp;q=%22John+the+Baptist%22&amp;tbs=bkt:s&amp;tbm=bks">newspapers</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1&amp;q=%22John+the+Baptist%22+-wikipedia">books</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22John+the+Baptist%22">scholar</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22John+the+Baptist%22&amp;acc=on&amp;wc=on">JSTOR</a></span></small></span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">February 2019</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Guido_D%27Arezzo" class="mw-redirect" title="Guido D&#39;Arezzo">Guido D'Arezzo</a> (991/992 – after 1033) an Italian Benedictine monk founded the standard music stave based on a hymn to Saint John the Baptist. The hymn that begins with <a href="/wiki/Ut_Queant_Laxis" class="mw-redirect" title="Ut Queant Laxis">Ut Queant Laxis</a> uses the first syllable for each line – Ut (later changed to Do), Re, Mi, Fa, Sol, La, Si. The teaching is also known as the <a href="/wiki/Solmization" title="Solmization">solmization</a> syllable.</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/This_Is_the_Record_of_John" title="This Is the Record of John">This Is the Record of John</a></i>, by <a href="/wiki/England" title="England">English</a> <a href="/wiki/Tudor_Period" class="mw-redirect" title="Tudor Period">Tudor</a> composer <a href="/wiki/Orlando_Gibbons" title="Orlando Gibbons">Orlando Gibbons</a> is a well-known part-setting of the <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_John" title="Gospel of John">Gospel of John</a> for solo voice, choir and <a href="/wiki/Pipe_organ" title="Pipe organ">organ</a> or <a href="/wiki/Viol" title="Viol">viol</a> accompaniment.</li>
<li>The reformer <a href="/wiki/Martin_Luther" title="Martin Luther">Martin Luther</a> wrote a <a href="/wiki/Hymn" title="Hymn">hymn</a> based on biblical accounts about the Baptist, "<span title="German-language text"><i lang="de"><a href="/wiki/Christ_unser_Herr_zum_Jordan_kam" title="Christ unser Herr zum Jordan kam">Christ unser Herr zum Jordan kam</a></i></span>" (1541), based for a cantata by Johann Sebastian Bach for the <a href="/wiki/Nativity_of_St_John_the_Baptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Nativity of St John the Baptist">feast day on 24 June</a>, <span title="German-language text"><span lang="de"><a href="/wiki/Christ_unser_Herr_zum_Jordan_kam,_BWV_7" title="Christ unser Herr zum Jordan kam, BWV 7"><i>Christ unser Herr zum Jordan kam</i>, BWV 7</a></span></span> (1724).</li>
<li><a href="/w/index.php?title=S._Giovanni_Battista&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="S. Giovanni Battista (page does not exist)"><i>S. Giovanni Battista</i></a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://imslp.org/wiki/S._Giovanni_Battista,_G.3.3_(Stradella,_Alessandro)" class="extiw" title="scores:S. Giovanni Battista, G.3.3 (Stradella, Alessandro)">scores</a>&#93;</span> (St. John the Baptist) is a 1676 <a href="/wiki/Oratorio" title="Oratorio">oratorio</a> by <a href="/wiki/Alessandro_Stradella" title="Alessandro Stradella">Alessandro Stradella</a>.</li>
<li>The well-known Advent hymn <i>On Jordan's Bank the Baptist's cry</i> was written by <a href="/wiki/Charles_Coffin_(writer)" title="Charles Coffin (writer)">Charles Coffin</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>John the Baptist (Jokanaan), Baritone, is a character in the opera <a href="/wiki/Salome_(opera)" title="Salome (opera)">Salome</a> by <a href="/wiki/Richard_Strauss" title="Richard Strauss">Richard Strauss</a>, premiered 1905 in Dresden. The text is from <a href="/wiki/Oscar_Wilde" title="Oscar Wilde">Oscar Wilde's</a> French poem, translated into German by <a href="/wiki/Hedwig_Lachmann" title="Hedwig Lachmann">Hedwig Lachmann</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>In popular music, <a href="/wiki/Bob_Dylan" title="Bob Dylan">Bob Dylan</a> dedicates four lines to John the Baptist in "<a href="/wiki/Tombstone_Blues" title="Tombstone Blues">Tombstone Blues</a>", the second track of his 1965 album <i><a href="/wiki/Highway_61_Revisited" title="Highway 61 Revisited">Highway 61 Revisited</a></i>. He sings: "John the Baptist after torturing a thief/Looks up at his hero the Commander-in-Chief/Saying, "Tell me great hero, but please make it brief/Is there a hole for me to get sick in?".<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The song "John the Baptist (Holy John)" by <a href="/wiki/Al_Kooper" title="Al Kooper">Al Kooper</a> on his 1971 album <i><a href="/wiki/New_York_City_(You%27re_a_Woman)" title="New York City (You&#39;re a Woman)">New York City (You're a Woman)</a></i> is about John the Baptist. In the same year the song was also recorded by <a href="/wiki/Blood,_Sweat_%26_Tears" title="Blood, Sweat &amp; Tears">Blood, Sweat &amp; Tears</a> for their album <i><a href="/wiki/Blood,_Sweat_%26_Tears_4" class="mw-redirect" title="Blood, Sweat &amp; Tears 4">Blood, Sweat &amp; Tears 4</a></i>.</li>
<li>The 1972 album <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Dreaming_with_Alice&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Dreaming with Alice (page does not exist)">Dreaming with Alice</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Mark_Fry" title="Mark Fry">Mark Fry</a> features several verses spread throughout the album retelling the story of the beheading of John the Baptist.</li>
<li>In his song "Everyman Needs a Companion", the closing track to his album <a href="/wiki/Fear_Fun" title="Fear Fun">Fear Fun</a>, <a href="/wiki/Josh_Tillman" class="mw-redirect" title="Josh Tillman">Father John Misty</a> sings about the friendship between John the Baptist and Jesus of Nazareth: "John the Baptist took Jesus Christ/Down to the river on a Friday night/They talked about Mary like a couple of boys/With nothing to lose/Too scared to try."<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>John the Baptist is referenced in the music of American <a href="/wiki/Heavy_metal_music" title="Heavy metal music">Heavy Metal</a> band <a href="/wiki/Om_(band)" title="Om (band)">Om</a> in their 2009 song 'Meditation is the Practice of Death'.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> As well as this, John the Baptist is depicted on the cover art of Om's 2012 album, <a href="/wiki/Advaitic_Songs" title="Advaitic Songs">Advaitic Songs</a>.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="In_film_and_television">In film and television</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: In film and television">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>John the Baptist has appeared in a number of screen adaptations of the life of Jesus. Actors who have played John include James D. Ainsley in <i><a href="/wiki/From_the_Manger_to_the_Cross" title="From the Manger to the Cross">From the Manger to the Cross</a></i> (1912), <a href="/wiki/Nigel_De_Brulier" title="Nigel De Brulier">Nigel De Brulier</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Salome_(1923_film)" class="mw-redirect" title="Salome (1923 film)">Salome</a></i> (1923), <a href="/wiki/Alan_Badel" title="Alan Badel">Alan Badel</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Salome_(1953_film)" title="Salome (1953 film)">Salome</a></i> (1953), <a href="/wiki/Robert_Ryan" title="Robert Ryan">Robert Ryan</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/King_of_Kings_(1961_film)" title="King of Kings (1961 film)">King of Kings</a></i> (1961),<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> Mario Socrate in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Gospel_According_to_St._Matthew_(film)" title="The Gospel According to St. Matthew (film)">The Gospel According to St. Matthew</a></i> (1964),<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Charlton_Heston" title="Charlton Heston">Charlton Heston</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Greatest_Story_Ever_Told" title="The Greatest Story Ever Told">The Greatest Story Ever Told</a></i> (1965),<sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/David_Haskell" title="David Haskell">David Haskell</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Godspell_(film)" title="Godspell (film)">Godspell</a></i> (1973),<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Michael_York_(actor)" class="mw-redirect" title="Michael York (actor)">Michael York</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Jesus_of_Nazareth_(miniseries)" class="mw-redirect" title="Jesus of Nazareth (miniseries)">Jesus of Nazareth</a></i> (1977), <a href="/wiki/Eli_Cohen_(actor)" title="Eli Cohen (actor)">Eli Cohen</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Jesus_(1979_film)" title="Jesus (1979 film)">Jesus</a></i> (1979),<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Andre_Gregory" class="mw-redirect" title="Andre Gregory">Andre Gregory</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Last_Temptation_of_Christ_(film)" title="The Last Temptation of Christ (film)">The Last Temptation of Christ</a></i> (1988),<sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> Christopher Routh in <i><a href="/wiki/Mary,_Mother_of_Jesus_(film)" title="Mary, Mother of Jesus (film)">Mary, Mother of Jesus</a></i> (1999), <a href="/wiki/David_O%27Hara" title="David O&#39;Hara">David O'Hara</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Jesus_(1999_film)" title="Jesus (1999 film)">Jesus</a></i> (1999), Scott Handy in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Gospel_of_John_(2003_film)" title="The Gospel of John (2003 film)">The Gospel of John</a></i> (2003), <a href="/wiki/Aidan_McArdle" title="Aidan McArdle">Aidan McArdle</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Judas_(2004_film)" title="Judas (2004 film)">Judas</a></i> (2004), <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Percival" title="Daniel Percival">Daniel Percival</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Son_of_God_(film)" title="Son of God (film)">Son of God</a></i> (2014) and <a href="/wiki/Abhin_Galeya" title="Abhin Galeya">Abhin Galeya</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Killing_Jesus_(2015_film)" title="Killing Jesus (2015 film)">Killing Jesus</a></i> (2015).
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/Snapaka_Yohannan" title="Snapaka Yohannan">Snapaka Yohannan</a></i> (<i>John the Baptist</i>), a 1963 Indian <a href="/wiki/Malayalam" title="Malayalam">Malayalam</a>-language film depicts life of St. John the Baptist and his death at the hands of <a href="/wiki/Salome" title="Salome">Salome</a>, <a href="/wiki/Herod_Antipas" title="Herod Antipas">Herod Antipas</a> and <a href="/wiki/Herodias" title="Herodias">Herodias</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-hindu_229-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hindu-229">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Commemoration">Commemoration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Commemoration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Denominational_festivals">Denominational festivals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Denominational festivals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Christian festivals associated with Saint John the Baptist and Forerunner are celebrated at various days by different denominations and are dedicated to his conception, <a href="/wiki/Nativity_of_Saint_John_the_Baptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Nativity of Saint John the Baptist">birth</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Beheading_of_Saint_John_the_Baptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Beheading of Saint John the Baptist">death</a>, as well as in correlation to the <a href="/wiki/Baptism_of_Jesus" title="Baptism of Jesus">baptism of Jesus</a>. The Eastern Church has feast days for the finding of his head (first, second, and third finding), as well as for his parents, Elizabeth and Zechariah. In the Russian Orthodox Church there is a feast day of the Transfer of the Right Hand of the Forerunner from Malta to Gatchina.<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;note 2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Association_with_summer_solstice">Association with summer solstice</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=48" title="Edit section: Association with summer solstice">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Feast of Saint John closely coincides with the <a href="/wiki/June_solstice" title="June solstice">June solstice</a>, also referred to as Midsummer in the Northern Hemisphere. The Christian holy day is fixed at 24 June; but in most countries festivities are mostly held the night before, on Saint John's Eve. "In England, 'Saint John's Tide' is combined with a midsummer celebration. Instead of the date of the summer solstice, they chose June 24. This may be because of the Baptist's own words, 'He must increase, but I must decrease' (John 3:30). John was, of course, referring to Jesus. John's day comes at the time when the sun is beginning to decrease..."<sup id="cite_ref-McNamara2000_232-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-McNamara2000-232">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Patron_saint_and_local_festivals">Patron saint and local festivals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: Patron saint and local festivals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main category: <a href="/wiki/Category:Saint_John%27s_Day" title="Category:Saint John&#39;s Day">Saint John's Day</a></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Middle_East">Middle East</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=50" title="Edit section: Middle East">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ein_Kerem_Church_of_St_John_the_Baptist_by_David_Shankbone.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Ein_Kerem_Church_of_St_John_the_Baptist_by_David_Shankbone.jpg/220px-Ein_Kerem_Church_of_St_John_the_Baptist_by_David_Shankbone.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Ein_Kerem_Church_of_St_John_the_Baptist_by_David_Shankbone.jpg/330px-Ein_Kerem_Church_of_St_John_the_Baptist_by_David_Shankbone.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Ein_Kerem_Church_of_St_John_the_Baptist_by_David_Shankbone.jpg/440px-Ein_Kerem_Church_of_St_John_the_Baptist_by_David_Shankbone.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="2448" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ein_Kerem_Church_of_St_John_the_Baptist_by_David_Shankbone.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Catholic church at his traditional birthplace in <a href="/wiki/Ein_Kerem" class="mw-redirect" title="Ein Kerem">Ein Kerem</a></div></div></div>
<p>Saint John the Baptist's beheading is said to have taken place in <a href="/wiki/Machaerus" title="Machaerus">Machaerus</a>, in central Jordan.<sup id="cite_ref-233" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-233">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-235" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-235">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Europe">Europe</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=51" title="Edit section: Europe">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:St_John_the_Baptist_Titular_Statue_of_Xewkija_Gozo,_Malta.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/St_John_the_Baptist_Titular_Statue_of_Xewkija_Gozo%2C_Malta.jpg/220px-St_John_the_Baptist_Titular_Statue_of_Xewkija_Gozo%2C_Malta.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/St_John_the_Baptist_Titular_Statue_of_Xewkija_Gozo%2C_Malta.jpg/330px-St_John_the_Baptist_Titular_Statue_of_Xewkija_Gozo%2C_Malta.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/St_John_the_Baptist_Titular_Statue_of_Xewkija_Gozo%2C_Malta.jpg/440px-St_John_the_Baptist_Titular_Statue_of_Xewkija_Gozo%2C_Malta.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:St_John_the_Baptist_Titular_Statue_of_Xewkija_Gozo,_Malta.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Wooden statue. <a href="/w/index.php?title=Pietro_Paolo_Azzopardi&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Pietro Paolo Azzopardi (page does not exist)">Pietro Paolo Azzopardi</a>, 1845, <a href="/wiki/Xewkija" title="Xewkija">Xewkija</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>In the United Kingdom, Saint John is the patron of <a href="/wiki/Penzance" title="Penzance">Penzance</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cornwall" title="Cornwall">Cornwall</a>. In <a href="/wiki/Scotland" title="Scotland">Scotland</a>, he is the patron saint of <a href="/wiki/Perth,_Scotland" title="Perth, Scotland">Perth</a>, which used to be known as St. John's Toun of Perth. The main church in the city is still the medieval Kirk of St. John the Baptist and the city's <a href="/wiki/Professional_sports" title="Professional sports">professional</a> <a href="/wiki/Football_club_(association_football)" title="Football club (association football)">football club</a> is called <a href="/wiki/St_Johnstone_F.C." title="St Johnstone F.C.">St Johnstone F.C.</a>
</p><p>Also, on the night of 23 June on to the 24th, Saint John is celebrated as the patron saint of <a href="/wiki/Porto" title="Porto">Porto</a>, the second largest city in <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>. An article from June 2004 in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Guardian" title="The Guardian">The Guardian</a></i> remarked that "Porto's <span title="Portuguese-language text"><i lang="pt"><a href="/wiki/Festa_de_S%C3%A3o_Jo%C3%A3o" class="mw-redirect" title="Festa de São João">Festa de São João</a></i></span> is one of Europe's liveliest street festivals, yet it is relatively unknown outside the country".<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As patron saint of the original Order of Knights of the Hospital of Saint John, he is the patron of the Knights Hospitaller of Jerusalem, <a href="/wiki/Malta" title="Malta">Malta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Florence" title="Florence">Florence</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cesena" title="Cesena">Cesena</a>, <a href="/wiki/Turin" title="Turin">Turin</a> and <a href="/wiki/Genoa" title="Genoa">Genoa</a>, Italy; as well as of Malta as a whole and of <a href="/wiki/Xewkija" title="Xewkija">Xewkija</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gozo" title="Gozo">Gozo</a> in Malta, which remember him with a great feast on the Sunday nearest to 24 June.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Americas">Americas</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=52" title="Edit section: Americas">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Saint John the Baptist is the <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Puerto_Rico" class="mw-redirect" title="Commonwealth of Puerto Rico">Commonwealth of Puerto Rico</a> and its capital city, <a href="/wiki/San_Juan,_Puerto_Rico" title="San Juan, Puerto Rico">San Juan</a>. In 1521, the island was given its formal name, <span title="Spanish-language text"><i lang="es">"San Juan Bautista de Puerto Rico"</i></span>, following the custom of christening a town with its formal name and the name which <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Columbus" title="Christopher Columbus">Christopher Columbus</a> had originally given the island. The names <span title="Spanish-language text"><i lang="es">"San Juan Bautista"</i></span> and <span title="Spanish-language text"><i lang="es">"Puerto Rico"</i></span> were eventually used in reference to both city and island, leading to a reversal in terminology by most inhabitants largely due to a <a href="/wiki/Cartography" title="Cartography">cartographic</a> error. By 1746, the city's name (<span title="Spanish-language text"><i lang="es">"Puerto Rico"</i></span>) had become that of the entire island, while the name for the island (<span title="Spanish-language text"><i lang="es">"San Juan Bautista"</i></span>) had become that of the city. The <a href="/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_mottos" class="mw-redirect" title="List of U.S. state mottos">official motto</a> of Puerto Rico also references the saint: <span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la">Joannes Est Nomen Eius</i></span>.<sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>He is also a patron saint of <a href="/wiki/French_Canada" class="mw-redirect" title="French Canada">French Canada</a> and <a href="/wiki/Newfoundland_and_Labrador" title="Newfoundland and Labrador">Newfoundland</a>. The Canadian cities of <a href="/wiki/St._John%27s,_Newfoundland_and_Labrador" title="St. John&#39;s, Newfoundland and Labrador">St. John's, Newfoundland</a> (1497), <a href="/wiki/Saint_John,_New_Brunswick" title="Saint John, New Brunswick">Saint John, New Brunswick</a> (1604), and <a href="/wiki/Saint-Jean-sur-Richelieu" title="Saint-Jean-sur-Richelieu">Saint-Jean-sur-Richelieu, Quebec</a> (1665), were all named in his honor. His feast day of 24 June is celebrated officially in <a href="/wiki/Quebec" title="Quebec">Quebec</a> as the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/F%C3%AAte_Nationale_du_Qu%C3%A9bec" class="mw-redirect" title="Fête Nationale du Québec">Fête Nationale du Québec</a></i></span> and was previously celebrated in Newfoundland as <a href="/wiki/Discovery_Day" title="Discovery Day">Discovery Day</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He is also patron of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Charleston" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Charleston">Roman Catholic Diocese of Charleston</a>, which covers the whole of <a href="/wiki/South_Carolina" title="South Carolina">South Carolina</a> in the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Southeast_Asia">Southeast Asia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=53" title="Edit section: Southeast Asia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p><a href="/wiki/Calamba_City,_Laguna" class="mw-redirect" title="Calamba City, Laguna">Calamba City, Laguna</a>, <a href="/wiki/Calumpit,_Bulacan" class="mw-redirect" title="Calumpit, Bulacan">Calumpit, Bulacan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Balayan,_Batangas" class="mw-redirect" title="Balayan, Batangas">Balayan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lian,_Batangas" title="Lian, Batangas">Lian</a> in <a href="/wiki/Batangas_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Batangas (province)">Batangas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sipocot" title="Sipocot">Sipocot</a> and <a href="/wiki/San_Fernando,_Camarines_Sur" title="San Fernando, Camarines Sur">San Fernando, Camarines Sur</a>, <a href="/wiki/Daet,_Camarines_Norte" class="mw-redirect" title="Daet, Camarines Norte">Daet, Camarines Norte</a> <a href="/wiki/San_Juan,_Metro_Manila" title="San Juan, Metro Manila">San Juan, Metro Manila</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tabuelan,_Cebu" class="mw-redirect" title="Tabuelan, Cebu">Tabuelan, Cebu</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Jimenez,_Misamis_Occidental" title="Jimenez, Misamis Occidental">Jimenez, Misamis Occidental</a> are among several places in the <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a> that venerate John as the town or city patron. A common practise of many <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_the_Philippines" title="Culture of the Philippines">Filipino fiestas</a> in his honour is bathing and the dousing of people in memory of John's iconic act. The custom is similar in form to <a href="/wiki/Songkran" title="Songkran">Songkran</a> and <a href="/wiki/Holi" title="Holi">Holi</a>, and serves as a playful respite from the intense tropical heat. While famed for the <a href="/wiki/Black_Nazarene" title="Black Nazarene">Black Nazarene</a> it enshrines, <a href="/wiki/Quiapo_Church" title="Quiapo Church">Quiapo Church</a> in <a href="/wiki/Manila" title="Manila">Manila</a> is actually dedicated to Saint John.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Orders_and_societies">Orders and societies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=54" title="Edit section: Orders and societies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Baptistines_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="Baptistines (disambiguation)">Baptistines</a> are the name given to a number of <a href="/wiki/Religious_orders" class="mw-redirect" title="Religious orders">religious orders</a> dedicated to the memory of John the Baptist.
</p><p>John the Baptist is the name-giving patron of the <a href="/wiki/Knights_Hospitaller" title="Knights Hospitaller">Knights Hospitaller</a>, or Knights of Saint John.<sup id="cite_ref-240" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-240">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Along with <a href="/wiki/John_the_Evangelist" title="John the Evangelist">John the Evangelist</a>, John the Baptist is claimed as a patron saint by the fraternal society of <a href="/wiki/Freemasonry" title="Freemasonry">Free and Accepted Masons</a> (better known as the Freemasons).<sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=55" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_the_Baptist&amp;action=edit&amp;section=57" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-19"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-19">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Greek_language" title="Greek language">Greek</a>: <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language text"><span lang="grc">Ἰωάννης ὁ βαπτιστής</span></span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Romanization_of_Greek" title="Romanization of Greek">translit.</a></small>&#160;<span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language romanization"><i lang="grc-Latn"><i>Iōánnēs ho baptistḗs</i></i></span> or <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language text"><span lang="grc">Ἰωάννης ὁ βαπτίζων</span></span>, <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language romanization"><i lang="grc-Latn">Iōánnēs ho baptízōn</i></span>, or <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language text"><span lang="grc">Ἰωάννης ὁ πρόδρομος</span></span>, <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language romanization"><i lang="grc-Latn">Iōánnēs ho pródromos</i></span>;<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-World_History_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-World_History-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Classical_Syriac_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Classical Syriac language">Classical Syriac</a>: <span lang="syc" dir="rtl">ܝܘܿܚܲܢܵܢ ܡܲܥܡܕ݂ܵܢܵܐ</span>, <small>romanized:&#160;</small><span title="Classical Syriac-language romanization"><i lang="syc-Latn">Yoḥanān Maʿmḏānā</i></span>; <a href="/wiki/Hebrew_language" title="Hebrew language">Hebrew</a>: <span lang="he" dir="rtl">יוחנן המטביל</span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Romanization_of_Hebrew" title="Romanization of Hebrew">romanized</a>:&#160;</small><span title="Hebrew-language romanization"><i lang="he-Latn">Yohanān HaMatbil</i></span>; <a href="/wiki/Latin_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin language">Latin</a>: <i lang="la">Ioannes Baptista</i>; <a href="/wiki/Coptic_language" title="Coptic language">Coptic</a>: <span lang="cop">ⲓⲱⲁⲛⲛⲏⲥ ⲡⲓⲡⲣⲟⲇⲣⲟⲙⲟⲥ</span> or <span title="Coptic-language text"><span lang="cop">ⲓⲱ̅ⲁ ⲡⲓⲣϥϯⲱⲙⲥ</span></span>;<sup id="cite_ref-st-takla.org_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-st-takla.org-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Arabic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Arabic language">Arabic</a>: <span lang="ar" dir="rtl">يوحنا المعمدان</span>;<sup id="cite_ref-st-takla.org_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-st-takla.org-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Classical_Mandaic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Classical Mandaic language">Classical Mandaic</a>: <span lang="myz" dir="rtl">ࡉࡅࡄࡀࡍࡀ ࡌࡀࡑࡁࡀࡍࡀ</span>, <small>romanized:&#160;</small><span title="Classical Mandaic-language romanization"><i lang="myz-Latn">Iuhana Maṣbana</i></span>.<sup id="cite_ref-GR_Gelbert_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GR_Gelbert-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The name "John" is the Anglicized form, via French, Latin and then Greek, of the Hebrew, "Yochanan",<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> which means "<a href="/wiki/God_in_Christianity" title="God in Christianity">YHWH</a> is gracious".<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>

</li>

The Fact That Jesus of Nazareth Was Not Accepted as Messiah Was Not Due to the People's Lack Of Faith In God. <a rel="nofollow" class="external free" href="https://www.tparents.org/Library/Unification/Publications/Other-Pub/Uc-jewsh.htm">https://www.tparents.org/Library/Unification/Publications/Other-Pub/Uc-jewsh.htm</a></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Verheyden-145"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Verheyden_145-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">J Verheyden, <i>Epiphanius on the Ebionites</i>, in <i>The image of the Judaeo-Christians in ancient Jewish and Christian literature</i>, eds Peter J. Tomson, Doris Lambers-Petry, <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/3-16-148094-5" title="Special:BookSources/3-16-148094-5">3-16-148094-5</a>, p. 188 "The vegetarianism of John the Baptist and of Jesus is an important issue too in the Ebionite interpretation of the Christian life. "</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-146"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-146">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Robert_Eisenman" title="Robert Eisenman">Robert Eisenman</a> (1997), <i><a href="/wiki/James_the_Brother_of_Jesus_(book)" title="James the Brother of Jesus (book)">James the Brother of Jesus</a></i>, p. 240 – "John (unlike Jesus) was both a '<a href="/wiki/Rechabite" class="mw-redirect" title="Rechabite">Rechabite</a>' or 'Nazarite' and vegetarian", p. 264 – "One suggestion is that John ate 'carobs'; there have been others. Epiphanius, in preserving what he calls 'the Ebionite Gospel', rails against the passage there claiming that John ate 'wild honey' and 'manna-like vegetarian cakes dipped in oil. ... John would have been one of those wilderness-dwelling, vegetable-eating persons", p. 326 – "They [the Nazerini] ate nothing but wild fruit milk and honey – probably the same food that John the Baptist also ate.", p. 367 – "We have already seen how in some traditions 'carobs' were said to have been the true composition of John's food.", p. 403 – "his [John's] diet was stems, roots and fruits. Like James and the other Nazirites/Rechabites, he is presented as a vegetarian ..".</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-147"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-147">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/James_Tabor" title="James Tabor">James Tabor</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Jesus_Dynasty" title="The Jesus Dynasty">The Jesus Dynasty</a></i> p. 134 and footnotes p. 335, p. 134 – "The Greek New Testament gospels says John's diet consisted of "locusts and wild honey" but an ancient Hebrew version of Matthew insists that "locusts" is a mistake in Greek for a related Hebrew word that means a cake of some type, made from a desert plant, similar to the "manna" that the ancient Israelites ate in the desert on the days of Moses.(ref 9) Jesus describes John as "neither eating nor drinking," or "neither eating bread nor drinking wine." Such phrases indicate the lifestyle of one who is strictly vegetarian, avoids even bread since it has to be processed from grain, and shuns all alcohol.(ref 10) The idea is that one would eat only what grows naturally.(ref 11) It was a way of avoiding all refinements of civilization."</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Ehrman_2003_on_Gospel_of_the_Ebionites-148"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Ehrman_2003_on_Gospel_of_the_Ebionites_148-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFBart_D._Ehrman2003" class="citation book cs1">Bart D. Ehrman (2003). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/lostchristianiti00ehrm/page/102"><i>Lost Christianities: The Battles for Scripture and the Faiths We Never Knew</i></a>. Oxford University Press. pp.&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/lostchristianiti00ehrm/page/102">102, 103</a>. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-0-19-514183-2" title="Special:BookSources/978-0-19-514183-2"><bdi>978-0-19-514183-2</bdi></a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Lost+Christianities%3A+The+Battles+for+Scripture+and+the+Faiths+We+Never+Knew&amp;rft.pages=102%2C+103&amp;rft.pub=Oxford+University+Press&amp;rft.date=2003&amp;rft.isbn=978-0-19-514183-2&amp;rft.au=Bart+D.+Ehrman&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Farchive.org%2Fdetails%2Flostchristianiti00ehrm%2Fpage%2F102&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AJohn+the+Baptist" class="Z3988"></span> p. 102 – "Probably the most interesting of the changes from the familiar New Testament accounts of Jesus comes in the Gospel of the Ebionites description of John the Baptist, who, evidently, like his successor Jesus, maintained a strictly vegetarian cuisine."</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-149"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-149">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">James A. <li id="cite_note-Mead_2007-150"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Mead_2007_150-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFG.R.S._Mead2007" class="citation book cs1">G.R.S. Forgotten Books. p.&#160;104. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-1-60506-210-5" title="Special:BookSources/978-1-60506-210-5"><bdi>978-1-60506-210-5</bdi></a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Gnostic+John+the+Baptizer%3A+Selections+from+the+Mand%C3%A6an+John-Book&amp;rft.pages=104&amp;rft.pub=Forgotten+Books&amp;rft.date=2007&amp;rft.isbn=978-1-60506-210-5&amp;rft.au=G.R.S.+Mead&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.sacred-texts.com%2Fchr%2Fgno%2Fgjb%2Fgjb-3.htm&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AJohn+the+Baptist" class="Z3988"></span> p. 104 – "And when he had been brought to Archelaus and the doctors of the Law had assembled, they asked him who he is and where he has been until then. <li id="cite_note-151"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-151">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Tabor (2006) <i>Jesus Dynasty</i> p. 334 (note 9) – "<i>The Gospel of the Ebionites</i> as quoted by the 4th-century writer Epiphanius. The Greek word for locusts (<i>akris</i>) is very similar to the Greek word for "honey cake" (<i>ekris</i>) that is used for the "manna" that the Israelites ate in the desert in the days of Moses (Exodus 16:32)" &amp; p. 335 (note 11) – "There is an old Russian (Slavic) version of Josephus's <i>Antiquities</i> that describes John the Baptizer as living on 'roots and fruits of the tree' and insists that he never touches bread, even at Passover."</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Ehrman_2003_Gospel_of_the_Ebionites_translation-152"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Ehrman_2003_Gospel_of_the_Ebionites_translation_152-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFBart_D._Ehrman2003" class="citation book cs1">Bart D. Their epithet is: "the Righteous." They form a connected group round Jesus. Zachariah was the father of John the Baptist, who is referenced as "Elias, which was for to come" (Matt 11:14); and Elias is said to have been present and talked to Jesus at the Transfiguration on the Mount (Matt. 17:3)."</span>
